## 2026-10-13: functional separate recovery cancellation (T073 partial)

Implemented the human-admin route
`POST /api/v1/admin/upgrades/recoveries/{recovery_id}/cancel`, strict shared
request/receipt contracts, operation-lock serialization, live administrator
authorization, exact intent binding and atomic cancellation/audit retention.
Another live administrator can revoke a demoted initiator's intent. Concurrent
and lost-response retries return the first cancellation without recapturing its
actor/time. Revocation changes only the separate phase to `recovery_required`;
original plans/deadlines, both active slots, maintenance fences, transfer,
consumption and owner preimages remain retained. Existing live support/consumption/
succession/successor gates refuse further effects. Historical replay is inspection.

Extended this feature's single 0034 migration and matching metadata with the
cancellation JSON column, immutable-history trigger and no-executable-reopening
constraint. Future qualified resolution can preserve the immutable cancellation
under existing recovered-phase requirements; cancellation alone never authorizes
resolution or terminalization. No live migration was performed. Existing native
legacy schema preparation remains a required supported deployment prerequisite.

panel, warning alert, promtool scenario, operator runbook and protocol description.
Regenerated OpenAPI and TypeScript. A failed route commit emits no cancellation
metric. Actual PostgreSQL exercises accepted/transferring/expired cancellation,
revocation of an accepted successor, demoted initiator, exact-hash refusal,
atomic audit failure, concurrent replay, preservation and blocked first consumption.
Physical/native facts in the owner fixtures remain synthetic; this is not native
handover or qualification evidence.

### Cross-kernel host ownership correction

An actual hardened, network-isolated container probe against the pinned existing local
Coire API image exposed non-propagating `flock` across macOS and OrbStack Linux: the
container acquired a lock while the host held it. This failed experiment invalidated the
local-lock-only approach. Deployment ownership now combines local `flock` with a typed,
private atomically published shared claim. Host children verify both inherited ownership
and the shared claim. Failed external mutations retain intent and claim; successful or
pre-effect completion releases the claim. Foreign-kernel abandoned claims require original
namespace recovery rather than PID or deadline inference. ADR-0015 records the finding.

The corrected actual host/container test verifies exclusion while the host owns the shared
claim and availability after committed release. Initial corrected run: 29 passed across
host ownership, deployment and the real Docker probe. Additional tests cover abandoned
claim retention and refusal of foreign-kernel takeover with a matching intent witness.
This evidence does not complete executor recovery, handover, Studio smoke or feature 019.

### Additional foundation admission evidence

The shared deployment claim suite, actual hardened Docker ownership probe and all upgrade
wire-contract checks passed together: 45 tests. Ruff and strict mypy passed for the affected
ownership modules. Synchronous node inventory now revalidates maintenance authority after
collectors, matching the asynchronous path; the release-during-collection regression failed
before the fix and passed afterward (10 maintenance tests). Prepared artifact reads now
reject bytes beyond the declared size and inode replacement rather than trusting an initial
`stat`, and retained Compose hashing bounds concurrent growth. Host CLI maps Coire errors
without exposing a traceback.

Queued training measurements now acquire the upgrade coordination lock before claim locks,
wait through Core maintenance, and recheck their target nodes under canonical admission
locks before allocating holds. Existing dispatch reconciliation and cleanup continue. The
new regression failed before implementation; 41 affected training/preference measurement
and Core admission tests passed afterward, with strict mypy passing. Evaluation measurement
claims receive the same Core/target-node barrier while durable DBOS waiting remains active.
These are foundation changes; no runtime activation or acceptance gate is claimed complete.

### Immutable artifact publication and further checks

The host preparation command `scripts/coire-deploy.py prepare-upgrade` now accepts only a
private typed operator document and explicit local artifact bindings, copies bounded stable
owned regular files into a temporary private catalog, verifies all full hashes/declared bytes,
fsyncs and publishes under exclusive ownership. Registration receives only the opaque
artifact ID and manifest hash. Existing releases refuse overwrite; failed copies remove only
the unpublished temporary directory. Preparation emits a redacted span, bounded outcome
metric and structured owner/release logs. CLI publication, immutable retry and hash-failure
cleanup tests pass; service/bootstrap/schema-specific provenance verification remains pending.

Retained interpreter duplicate-field regression failed before correction; afterward 25
installer unit tests plus the actual isolated native interpreter retention test passed (26,
3.86 seconds). Shared private journals now also reject duplicate authority fields, oversized
reads and identity changes. The ownership context verifies the latest owner before commit,
so a changed owner cannot be overwritten by context exit. The combined journal, maintenance,
host ownership and actual Docker probe suite passed: 32 tests. A broader focused regression
passed 135 tests before the latest artifact-publication and dataset-admission additions.

Full strict type checking passed for 1044 sources with the repository CI command
`uv run mypy apps/ packages/ scripts/coire-deploy.py`. OpenAPI freshness passed with
`uv run python -m coire_api.openapi --check`. The planning quickstart's no-target mypy and
nonexistent `coire-api export-openapi` alias were corrected to these actual repository
entrypoints. Formatting/lint findings were corrected rather than suppressed. None of these
checks fulfills the remaining implementation, image, engine or real-cluster release gates.

same ordered locks. Accepted feedback regeneration waits for a permitted Core transaction
without retaining user locks during the wait, then rechecks live owner authority before
claiming. Existing generation/accounting/cleanup behavior remains exercised by 11 feedback
comparison contract cases. Core-admission plus comparison contracts passed: 16 tests; run
placement plus Core-admission regression passed: 10 tests.

Core publication/registration now verifies `compose.json` full bytes against both declared
hashes, exact project, all present fixed-cohort roles, each service configuration and image

hash or hash-valid mismatched service configuration now refuse publication. The combined
artifact and host deployment suite passed: 25 tests. Docker platform/bootstrap/source-mount
and supported-config preflight remain additional work, not inferred from this check.

Normal engine and image lifecycle owners perform termination and retain reservations
until positive process death. Loading engines, active requests/jobs, terminal cleanup,

Added a strict native selection receipt, binding the node, full release/runtime identities

Native guard journal foundation now retains exact accepted authority and Core-consumed

Authenticated node status/execute/cancel/maintenance transport is registered only on

The independent guard's fixed same-node observation transport now enforces credentials,

Serving child ownership is now journaled before its accepted input is delivered. The

Actual CPU subprocess pipe tests prove bounded output, nonzero-exit refusal and a blocked

Additional metadata/fault tests prove identity-publication-before-input, lost response,

Native activation refreshes source/peer drain after complete graph verification, retains

Maintenance scope checks now share one strict frozen-plan comparison. Process inventory

Durable preparation, consumed activation, candidate-serving, cancellation-to-restoration

A drain-timeout/cancellation abort must not keep healthy pre-existing work fenced until

### Executor resource reconciliation and independent intent foundation

Extended the executor's fixed read boundary to accepted Source Compose service names,
with exact project, role, image, network and container identity checks. Frozen service
translation preserves topology, existing physical named-volume identities and immutable
secret generations; named volumes use no-copy and binds prohibit mountpoint creation.
Unsupported or changed mounts, dependencies, shell healthchecks, unresolved environment,
unbounded logging/resource settings and namespace/capability additions are refused.
The prepared Compose reader rechecks accepted catalog metadata and full artifact bytes;
physical resource bindings derive from this frozen document, never public caller paths.
Actual Source configuration comparison includes immutable image defaults and refuses
execution, environment, healthcheck, resource, namespace and network drift.

Added an independent Core executor journal with immutable accepted-plan binding,
fsynced effect intents, exact-identity observation closure and retained uncertainty after
lost responses. Runtime, outage and recovery deadlines survive replay/restart; an ongoing
outage supplies the earlier recovery budget anchor. Candidate effects are refused once
recovery starts. Runtime continuation after migration requires retained terminal zero-exit
expected-schema evidence; migration image configuration comes from the hash-verified
fixed coire-migrate artifact. This is journal/preflight implementation, not a completed
rollout state machine or production migration acceptance.

Added kernel-backed executor host ownership checks. Effects must use the held descriptor
and exact durable project/job/generation/source/boot witness. A second writer is refused;
closing an active owner leaves its durable intent and shared lease retained. A changed or
abandoned owner requires reconciliation rather than PID/expiry guessing. Fixed Docker
mutations and verified bootstrap handover remain to be wired; readiness remains false.

Validation: 73 executor tests pass, including 31 frozen translation/Source drift tests,
25 fixed Docker read-boundary tests, 10 journal restart/uncertainty tests, five ownership
checks and two readiness checks. Targeted strict mypy and Ruff pass. Added prepared-Compose
reader checks to the artifact suite. Existing scheduler revocation reconciliation now
retains ownership on node transport failures rather than escaping its retry loop. No
executor Compose network membership, proxy flag, public port, production credential,
real Studio workload or production schema was changed by this increment.

### Fixed Source stop and service-owned catalog publication

The fixed Source-stop method now requires its exact durable effect, current kernel
owner, hash-verified Source artifact, matching physical container/config/image defaults,
and the original readiness/outage deadline. It persists transport uncertainty before
sending its sole accepted POST stop request. Only fresh stopped-state observation closes
the effect; a lost response is reconciled without another stop, while a still-running
uncertain outcome remains blocking. Source containers and their volume references are
retained. Six mocked transport cases pass: normal closure, wrong identity, configuration
drift, lost response after stopping, refusal of blind retry, and expired outage. This
method remains unwired to managed upgrade acceptance pending the full executor/bootstrap.

Resolved the host/service UID mismatch for private prepared catalogs with a fixed
non-root stdin publication helper and trusted host CLI mode. The helper receives no
filesystem paths, networking, credentials or Docker socket. It verifies bounded artifact
bytes and immutable publication; API registration remains separately authenticated and
audited. Seven publication integrity tests and five host Docker-boundary tests pass.
The current executor image includes a private prepared-volume seed owned by UID 65532.

Actual isolated Linux ARM64 container publication PASS: built executor image
`sha256:d2274ce9d6214031d6af6b2ed93dd724b5cbc0c933111033fb8c0ed577218f37`
(as reported by Docker image inspect), created a unique disposable project/catalog volume,
transferred Core Compose metadata through the hardened network-none stdin helper, then
verified exact manifest/artifact bytes from a second read-only non-root container.
Observed artifact owner UID 65532 and mode 0600; zero MLX imports. The isolated volume
was removed. This verifies catalog UID/permission interoperability only; no production
catalog, managed bootstrap, actual release activation or real-Studio acceptance occurred.

Combined deployment, publication, prepared-artifact and executor checks: 125 tests pass.
Full strict mypy passes across 1073 source files. Current executor image builds successfully
for Linux ARM64. Network memberships remain unchanged and managed readiness remains false.

### Native no-claim closure and delayed abort bounds

A fenced peer or unstarted Source can no longer treat a missing local claim as proof
that operation data and disk ownership are absent. Matching unpublished operation
paths, reservation paths and symlinks block fence release until reconciliation; the
check does not create directories or delete unknown data. Terminal checks reject a
missing bootstrap or another active native claim. Before publishing a later claim,
the controller re-verifies the previous released operation's terminal cleanup. Native
commands/cancellations were confirmed to already be isolated within each operation;
no global signal-retirement mechanism was needed or retained.

Unchanged-Source abort capture now uses the original drain expiry, recorded candidate
failure, or private cancellation marker time rather than granting a fresh budget when
a delayed/restarted guard first observes the cause. New late-drain and late-cancellation
cases retain recovery-required fencing with no status/inventory/effect calls after the
original 300-second budget. Four absent/unpublished-resource cases and a higher-generation
active-owner refusal test also pass. Focused native terminal/unchanged closure cohort:
21 tests pass; node route cohort additionally passes. This does not complete unstarted
coordinator closure or real native acceptance; those remain required work.

### Continued runtime qualification, bootstrap and unstarted cancellation

Tokenizer analysis now verifies the sealed installed graph before any MLX import and
uses its full SHA in managed mode. Training and analysis workers receive only the
three runtime identity settings; control credentials are excluded. Current training
profile lookup, protective matching and measurement acceptance reject changed, unknown,
stale or future Studio runtime observations while retaining historical reports.
Focused tokenizer/worker tests: 51 passed; training specification/measurement/profile
unit cohort: 65 passed; qualification fault cases: 6 passed.

Managed link probes use each Studio's `envs/current/bin/python3` and derive that rank's
local Studio identity before graph verification and MLX import. Remote rank identity
is no longer dependent on `mlx.launch` forwarding the parent's settings. Six link
probe unit tests pass, including a CPU-only remote-rank refusal before native import.
No real collective was run by these tests; paired upgrade acceptance remains required.

The node installer now offers a separate `--bootstrap-upgrades` operation on an already
installed Source. It verifies prepared Source metadata and renders both a retained guard
plist and an identity-enabled node plist without starting/stopping services. Existing
managed ownership blocks direct installer activation; stage-only preparation remains
available. Installer/bootstrap cohort: 37 passed; shell syntax and strict typing pass.
Real guard installation and drained startup acceptance remain pending.

Unstarted cancellation now persists an operation-scoped terminal refusal only after

local resources remain blockers and are not deleted. The Studio coordinator supplies
the retained untouched plan for pre-delivery cancellation/revocation, and checks a
pending cancellation before dispatching preparation. Native cancellation/terminal,
node routes, wire models and coordinator cohort: 53 passed. Partial metadata recovery,
real PostgreSQL coordinator races and native workload acceptance remain required.

The fixed Core Docker boundary also restores the exact retained Source container under
the captured recovery budget, with configuration/mount verification, durable uncertainty
before HTTP, and positive running/healthy observation afterward. Completion replay
rechecks current state. Stop/start lost-response and journal cohort: 23 passed, strict

### Core executor snapshot and orchestration progress

Added strict retained Source cohort snapshots and read-only whole-cohort Docker
preflight. Recovery identity is fsynced only after complete frozen configuration,
physical mount/network, healthy Source and immutable ARM64 target-image validation.
Restart tests reject changed container/image identity, incomplete cohorts, stale
observations and future observations. No Docker mutation occurs during preflight.

The fixed one-shot migration transport now handles lost create/start replies with
exact-resource reconciliation. The executor client validates claim, receipt, grant,
status and migration-proof acknowledgments, including bounded/duplicate JSON and
redirect rejection. Activation consumption supports exact durable acknowledgment
replay without issuing a new grant or extending a deadline.

Added executor orchestration for migration proof aggregation, ordered cohort
replacement and reverse-order Source restoration. Tests cover all six roles, partial
replacement failure, exact-Source final verification, terminal migration failure,
schema-proof mismatch and replay without repeating DDL. The overall schema effect
is recorded uncertain before migration start; terminal zero plus exact schema proof

### Operator interfaces, retained Source and handover journal progress

Added the strict asynchronous upgrade SDK and API-only CLI, with one POST per
invocation, explicit maintenance acknowledgment, preserved idempotency keys and
bounded status/event reconnect. Studio manual rollback uses the successful parent
operation's retained Source and repeats fresh acceptance checks. Console capability
is reauthorized on every snapshot response, outside the shared cache. The console
uses generated types and the shared SSE hook, persists an uncertain mutation before
POST, and can explicitly retry the same key after reload. SSE retry jitter is capped
at ten seconds. Successful rollback is distinguished from successful upgrade;
uncertain cleanup keeps recovery and maintenance visible.

Released Studio status independently verifies retained Source bytes before exposing
its operation link. Target projection offers rollback only for a matching successful
parent and compatible freshly verified Source. Incompatible rollback has a separate
blocker and does not disable otherwise compatible forward upgrades.

Core readiness is bounded per service, with a captured aggregate cohort limit and
an independent gateway outage cap. Restart cannot renew a service window. Unclaimed
native preparation permits independently verified Source startup under its existing
maintenance fence; an accepted or ambiguous claim remains blocked. Native smoke
checks the actual Studio hostname before constructing an engine.

Added the durable executor handover journal: passive verification, fenced claim,
original 120-second stability window, incumbent restoration and monotonic subsequent
handover. Journal methods do not start containers, advertise readiness, or substitute
for actual incumbent supervision and sole-writer locking; runtime integration is still
required, so T041 remains open.

### Protected backup preparation and Core acceptance progress

Implemented `scripts/coire-deploy.py prepare-backup`: a bounded Source dump, restore
into a disconnected disposable PostgreSQL 17 container using the existing immutable
Source image, and comparison of the exact same structural schema digest used by the
activation gate. The Source is read only; credentials and production database state
are not changed. The isolated restore container has read-only root, no network,
no capabilities, tmpfs data and explicit resource limits. Cleanup reinspects its exact
ID/name/image/owner label before stopping/removing it. Dump and restore data each have
a tested 4 GiB bound; publication additionally requires dump size plus 1 GiB staging
headroom. Unsupported larger databases fail rather than evicting retained assets.

Protected backup metadata is strict shared Pydantic wire data. Preparation takes the
shared deployment lease, refuses active/uncertain ownership and existing managed holds,
and leaves the selected owner unchanged. A fixed binary stream publisher copies verified
dump bytes into the existing prepared volume under UID 65532 without a shell, credentials,
Docker socket or network. Only a complete verified publication updates `backups/latest.json`.
Replay checks immutable contents instead of overwriting a prior backup. Acceptance freezes
the selected receipt; both migration grant boundaries verify private dump ownership,
actual bytes, isolated restore receipt, deployed schema identity and 24-hour freshness.
Consumption rechecks grant expiry after prerequisite validation.

Core start and manual retained-source rollback now use fresh executor ownership,
exact observed Source/bootstrap checks, actual prepared artifacts and the shared acceptance
service. Core target remains not ready until runtime bootstrap is actually implemented;
these routes cannot enable it by themselves. Added accepted-operation-scoped read-only
Core inventory transport and client validation for Source, project, generation and
five-second freshness; complete transport does not imply idle ownership.

### Core ownership, drain boundaries and receipt replay — 2026-10-11

Core release preparation now binds the published manifest to an explicit owned
`host_release_key` and refuses absent, linked or changed host Compose bytes. Core
runtime observations can retain the exact container names and origin labels selected
by an earlier managed upgrade; replacement verification produces a whole-cohort
selection proof while retaining the active kernel owner. Historical migration
selection and durable selection commit remain pending.

Both Core activation boundaries collect strict live Core and Studio inventories,
including cleanup and renewal obligations. Busy, unknown, stale, future and foreign
observations refuse grants. A local receipt replay attempt has a ten-second ceiling,
requires the exact uploaded page's durable acknowledgment, and never deletes evidence.
Restart and lost-acknowledgment tests replay the same retained bytes.

The scheduler now dispatches a Core DBOS reconciliation workflow that establishes
durable Core fences, obtains exact peer acknowledgments and reconciles idle resident
work through authenticated node maintenance. Terminal fence release requires a
matching committed executor ownership and host selection receipt. It cannot infer
API-local active request state; the API collects that inventory at grant boundaries.
Cancelled or newly revoked accepted Core plans can obtain their restricted cleanup
owner, with cancellation persisted and audited; activation grants remain separately
authorized. End-to-end Core worker/bootstrap/commit wiring remains open.

### Core step engine and durable selection boundary (2026-10-11 UTC)

Added a finite Core step engine in `coire_upgrade_executor/workflow.py`. It freezes
Source before candidate preflight, preserves consumed activation intents and lost
acknowledgments, and runs physical restoration before API reconnection. API loss
during verification cannot renew the captured runtime deadline. Its tests exercise
the actual API progress validator with mocked physical effects and serving probes;
they are orchestration tests, not deployment or tiny-model acceptance.

The earlier terminal-first Target publication description is superseded. Target
now requires an ingested and acknowledged `committing`/`complete` decision bound to
prior serving evidence. Cancellation is serialized with that decision. Runtime,
host-selection and committed ownership files publish before `succeeded`; the API
independently validates their matching release, generation, service origins and
retained Source/historical migration references. Source recovery publication still
requires its ingested terminal progress. Published ownership prohibits additional
Docker effects. Interrupted publication stays fenced and requires reconciliation.

Added the additive `cancellation_allowed` capability, excluded from accepted
authority hashes. Terminal normalization respects frozen wire models. The console
disables cancellation after the decision and explains finalization. Validation also
rejects activation-complete labels without a consumed intent and requires the actual
schema structural digest, not just its head, before post-migration runtime authority.

Restart tests preserve the first physical-selection bytes across an explicitly
reconciled ownership generation; changing a container identity remains forbidden.
Target publication tests reject missing acknowledgment and validate the published
records through the real API success guard while retaining historical migration
artifacts. These are private filesystem/transport fixtures, not process-crash or
real Docker acceptance.

### Exact passive protocol and atomic transfer authority

The incumbent's passive HTTP check resolves only the candidate's Docker-attested
IPv4 address on the already verified internal network ID. It refuses public,
loopback/link-local, wrong-network, malformed or oversized responses and duplicate
JSON authority fields. Before/after Docker reads revalidate full candidate
configuration, unchanged process birth, positive health and unchanged address/network.

### Independent handover restoration boundaries

Added fixed, typed incumbent recovery with one original failure-detection time
and five-minute deadline. The private journal fsyncs candidate-stop dispatch once;
lost responses are reconciled through positive physical stopped state and cannot
renew dispatch or the deadline. Full prepared configuration/image/network/mount
inspection remains required even when the candidate is stopped or unhealthy.
The retained incumbent alone receives this fixed stop boundary; application,
foreign executor, changed hold, and other handover identities refuse it.

Incumbent reclamation requires a genuinely free kernel, another fresh candidate
stop observation after acquiring it, whole-Source physical health, unchanged
recovery authority and a new generation. An active abandoned candidate ownership
record can be reconciled; a genuinely held candidate kernel or a restarted
candidate cannot. Reclamation still grants no readiness or selection and keeps
the durable deployment hold. Restoration acknowledgment is an authenticated
executor-only typed route with fresh private stop evidence and a held incumbent
writer, serialized with accepted SQL authority. Necessary restoration survives
human revocation. Exact retry closes one SQL row with one audit entry.

Local finalization retains the acknowledged API result before recording restored
phase and releasing its exact deployment hold. An interruption during hold release
retains the result and original recovery authority for replay. It never selects
the candidate or replaces another selected owner.

### Supervisor routing, serialized restoration, and predecessor retirement

Connected the passive candidate, consumed writer transfer, independent Source
stability observer, SQL decision/qualification/final acknowledgment and local hold
release to executor startup. A passive startup creates no API client before a
consumed transfer. Source readiness is cleared before completed kernel release;
candidate readiness stays false until normal selected bootstrap qualification.
Source restart cannot resume the original candidate stability epoch. Immutable
commit proof is retained before dispatch for lost-response replay.

Restoration now captures failure detection before network work and requires a
durable restricted SQL `restoring` decision before candidate stop. It uses the
same operation lock as final commit, preserving published transfer/commit payloads
and auditing once. Terminal committed selection denies restoration. Missing status
or acknowledgment retains the hold and gives no stop permission. Final restoration
acknowledgment requires that serialized decision. Candidate reboot after SQL commit
captures a fresh kernel generation, qualifies its fresh heartbeat, and reconciles
only the exact interrupted private completion without renewing stability.

Predecessor retirement requires its SQL/private committed acknowledgment, released
exact hold, actual selected candidate writer, and bounded authenticated readiness
at the Docker-attested private IPv4. Changed address/process, foreign/oversized
reply, unready candidate, changed writer and uncertain stop refuse cleanup claims.
Only the exact old container is stopped; it and its image are retained. Stop intent
is fsynced once; uncertain dispatch cannot be repeated blindly. Successor preparation
archives the prior terminal generation and requires its retained acknowledgment
and selected predecessor identity before replacing the global preparation.

failure evidence, and preserves prior terminal acknowledgments. Bootstrap still
refuses active application owners, foreign incarnations and uncertain candidate
history. Source restarts advance writer generation; they never resume an older
candidate epoch. The focused owner/startup/preparation regression passed 38 tests.

### Accepted native V12: forward success and rollback fault evidence

The private Core-SQL acceptance admitted forward operation
`01M4P3DN0F7TJW2XE514WM0GHE`. It completed ten actual SQL receipts, real
64-token tiny-model pre/post serving with positive process cleanup, and three
actual JACCL observations at 9.254, 9.238 and 9.246 GB/s. The exact candidate
runtime was `dcd5d38688c8e8ff43cd1579d90378c05cdaf775d32df279625d840d9a65613c`;
the unchanged peer was
`3b01f78a517a2064d095e796a8ae1b679e9123d26b6f7e8405901ed0779d0c9e`.
The operation reached `succeeded`. The test nevertheless failed: an admin
status read timed out on an identity-row lock held across collective workers.
This is not a passing end-to-end acceptance test.

The normal admin rollback request admitted
`01M4P3S07YH888J8XPH6QZH6XS`. After two real collective workers, a node
status read collided with a short receipt-journal write and returned 409.
The guard independently restored that operation's Source (the forward candidate),
verified real tiny-model serving and cleanup, and reached `rolled_back` with 11
actual SQL receipts. Paired qualification was false and a failed qualification
observation was inserted before maintenance release, invalidating the former
pair's eligibility. Both actual maintenance rows were released. This is a safely
recovered failed manual rollback, not proof of successful manual rollback.
Original SQL evidence is retained privately in
`/private/tmp/coire-019-native-v12-core/api/actual-sql-evidence.json`.

Corrections release identity-row locks before remote collective work and
recheck live authority afterward, retain each real observation under fresh
checks, map failed pair reads to bounded Coire errors, and allow bounded
one-second waits for short journal/initial-intent writes. Lifetime recovery
ownership remains immediately exclusive; original deadlines are unchanged.
The SQL revocation regression uses actual PostgreSQL identity locks and proves
revocation completes during a dispatched worker and prevents evidence retention
(1 passed). The focused feature regression before journal wait changes passed
859 tests; journal/guard regression after the correction passed 34 tests.
A corrected immutable V13 fixture is being prepared for complete acceptance;
T028/T034 remain unchecked.

### Fixed passive delivery and configured support entrypoint

Core passive delivery now supplies explicit fresh pending/consumed state through
a fixed credentialed Node callback. Positive pending is restricted to live initial
accepted/transferring authority; consumed delivery binds exact intent/support

### Current helper restart revealed T076

Operation `01M4X1YQPYNA2M7XZ4BY8K330K`, generation 78, genuinely retired its
recorded guard and started a new independent guard. Source generated 64 fresh
tokens and positive cleanup at 11:18:17.305799 UTC, but its proof recorded
`collective_verified=false`; no Source collective dispatch was retained. The
original recovery deadline 11:22:51.064212 UTC elapsed, and the operation remains
`recovery_required`. The subsequent cancellation/revocation consumers refused
on this retained fence; their three-case batch failed and is preserved.

Code inspection found that Source qualification caught `UpgradeCollectivePending`
under its base `UpgradeUnavailable` handler and converted an unavailable owner
observation into a failed qualification. Source now mirrors candidate handling:
pending returns without a complete receipt or intent advancement, retaining the
original recovery deadline and exact smoke/collective scope. Regression coverage
now includes both Source and candidate success, definite failure and expiry;
all 14 guard workflow cases pass. Failed generation 78 is not rewritten. A new
scoped recovery API is bound exclusively to that original job/generation/Source,
using the incumbent's existing networks and hardened container policy. Immutable
corrected support is being staged without selecting it. Any distinct captured
recovery budget is explicitly authorized by the owner's standing instruction
to complete implementation without further approval prompts; the implementation
still requires authenticated, audited, non-renewing intent acceptance.
